[^372]: **Whoever knows to sequence the arrangement of his Lord…** On the phrase “to sequence the arrangement,” a key concept in the Zoharic *Heikhalot*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244b|2:244b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-259b|259b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-261b|261b]] (all *Heikh*).

    On first expressing praise before proceeding to prayer, see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 32#32a|32a]], in the name of Rabbi Simlai: “One should always array the praise of the blessed Holy One and then pray.” In that statement, according to Rashi (on BT *Avodah Zarah* [[Avodah Zarah 7#7b|7b]], s.v. *yesadder adam*), “praise” refers to the first three blessings of the *Amidah*, while “prayer” refers to various requests that follow. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169a|1:169a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-243b|243b]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-195b|3:195b]], [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-260b|260b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285a|285a]]. On the primacy of the first three blessings, see 2:261b (*Heikh*).

    In rabbinic literature the phrase ישראל‎ כנסת (*Keneset Yisra’el*), “Assembly of Israel,” nearly always denotes the people of Israel. The Midrash on the Song of Songs describes an allegorical love affair between the maiden (the earthly community of Israel) and her lover (the blessed Holy One). In the *Zohar, Keneset Yisra’el* can refer to the earthly community but also (often primarily) to *Shekhinah*—the divine feminine counterpart of the people, the aspect of God most intimately connected with them. The lovers in the Song of Songs are pictured as a divine couple, *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*.

    Thus the verse from Song of Songs alludes to *Shekhinah* embraced by *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, the divine right and left hands or arms, south and north respectively, through Her union with *Tif’eret*. On this verse, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49a|1:49a]]–b, [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-133a|133a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-136a|136a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-151a|151a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-163b|163b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-245a|245a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-250a|250a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-30a|2:30a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-169b|169b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-238b|238b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-26a|3:26a]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-55a|55a]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 63.

    In the daily morning liturgy, the blessing “Blessed are You, *YHVH*, who has redeemed Israel” immediately precedes the *Amidah*—the “standing prayer” that is so central that it is known simply as Prayer. It is considered meritorious to join the blessing of Redemption to the *Amidah* without any interruption or pause. Here, this joining symbolizes the union of the divine couple, *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*, which is consummated during the *Amidah*.

    On the importance of joining Redemption to Prayer, see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 4#4b|4b]], 9b, 10b. On its kabbalistic significance, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-132b|1:132b]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-205b|205b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-228b|228b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-128b|2:128b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-138b|138b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-156a|156a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-216b|216b]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-195b|3:195b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 78–79; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 75–76 (96).

[^373]: **Aspiration of prayer must ascend…** “Aspiration,” “intention,” and “desire” all render רעותא (*re’uta*). The opening sentence might also be construed: “Aspiration of prayer must ascend, binding Her above unto Infinity”—with “Her” signifying *Shekhinah*.

    On the expression “unto Infinity,” see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-45b|1:45b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244b|2:244b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-259b|259b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-260b|260b]] (all *Heikh*). See also 2:213b; Valabregue-Perry, *Ba-Nistar u-va-Nigleh*, 161–166.

    On intention, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99b|1:99b–100a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-161a|161a]]; [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-180b|2:180b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-200b|200b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-250a|250a]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-262b|262b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-184a|3:184a]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:952–53.

    The phrase “All is in mystery—revealed and secret” might mean that whereas the word (the words of prayer for example) is revealed, the mystical intention is secret.

[^374]: ***crowning him with favor*…** The righteous who fulfill God’s will [Aramaic: רעותא (*re’uta*)] are rewarded with favor [Hebrew: רצון (*ratson*), also meaning “will”].

[^375]: **Rabbi Shim’on opened…** Here begins the account of the seven halls of impurity, paralleling the halls of holiness. The shorter version of the *Heikhalot* found in *Parashat Be-Reshit* does not contain these halls. At the beginning of the halls of holiness in *Pequdei* (*Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245a|2:245a]]; above, page 55), Rabbi Shim’on likewise begins by addressing Adam.

    On Adam’s failure to fulfill the one commandment with which he was enjoined—namely not to eat from the tree of knowledge of good and evil—see *Bereshit Rabbah* 21:7; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 25:2; *Tanḥuma, Shemini* 8. On the phrase “Who will uncover the dust from your eyes,” see also M *Sotah* 5:2; Scholem, “Parashah Ḥadashah,” 433; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-41a|1:41a]] (*Heikh*). The sense of the phrase here is: would that you were still living to witness the effect of your actions!

    On the verse from Hosea, see BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 38#38b|38b]]; Rashi, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|1:56a]]; [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-122a|3:122a]]. The verse is cited in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-38a|1:38a]], at the beginning of *Heikhalot Bereshit*.

    As becomes clear below, the *serpent* signifies the demonic Other Side. On the verse from Genesis, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|49b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-52a|52a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-18c|18c]] (*MhN*). On the wicked words or evil speech of the serpent in the Garden of Eden, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 19:4; 20:1–2; *Devarim Rabbah* 5:10; *Tanḥuma, Ḥuqqat* 19; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Ḥuqqat* 45; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 19:22; *Midrash Tehillim* 1:9; *Shemot Rabbah* 3:12; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-148b|2:148b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-46b|3:46b]].

[^376]: ***there is but a step between me and death*…** In Kabbalah, David symbolizes *Malkhut*, whose realm borders the demonic realm of the Other Side. Even as David was banished from the holy land (signifying *Malkhut*) and descended from his rungs, he did not enter the Other Side.

    The verse from Samuel is spoken by David to Jonathan. David’s having been “banished” refers to his flight from King Saul. See 1 Samuel 19–21. On David outside the holy land, cf. BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 110#110b|110b]]. See also *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-8b|1:8b]], where David describes the gap separating himself from the Other Side as “a thread as fine as a filament of hair.”

    The description of David’s being “thrust in the font of flowing waters” refers to David (*Malkhut*) as being rooted in the divine flow above. See *Matoq mi-Devash*; *Sullam*. Cf. the account of David and the primal waters of the deep in BT *Sukkah* [[Sukkah 53#53a|53a]]–b.

[^377]: **seven names corresponding to the seven rungs of the halls…** The evil impulse and Hell each have seven names, which are bestowed in turn upon the seven halls of impurity.

    See BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 16#16a|16a]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘Satan, the evil impulse, and the Angel of Death are one and the same.” See also *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-52a|52a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-202a|202a]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 394–5. On the writhing serpent, see [[Isaiah 27#^27-1|Isaiah 27:1]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|2:265a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266a|266a]] (both *Heikh*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-18c|18c]] (*MhN*).

    On the seven names of the evil impulse, see BT *Sukkah* [[Sukkah 52#52a|52a]]: “Rabbi Avira, or some say Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Levi expounded, ‘The evil impulse has seven names. The blessed Holy One called it Evil…; Moses called it Uncircumcised…; David called it Impure…; Solomon called it Enemy…; Isaiah called it Stumbling Block…; Ezekiel called it Stone…; Joel called it Hidden One.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|1:18a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*), employing the same names as in the *Heikhalot* passage; Moses de León, *Mishkan ha-Edut*, 42b.

    On the seven names of Hell, see BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 19#19a|19a]], in the name of Rabbi Yehoshu’a: “Hell has seven names: Sheol, Ruin, Pit of Destruction, Tumultuous Pit, Miry Clay, Shadow of Death, and Netherworld.” See also *Midrash Tehillim* 11:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|1:18a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*), employing the same names as in this passage; Moses de León, *Mishkan ha-Edut*, 42b.

[^378]: **just as there are rungs and halls on the Side of Holiness…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244a|2:244a]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:450–51.

[^379]: **One who comes to purify himself…** See BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 104#104a|104a]], in the name of Resh Lakish: “… If one comes to defile himself, he is provided an opening; if one comes to purify himself, he is given assistance.” See also BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 38#38b|38b]]–39a. “Mystery of Faith” may designate either *Malkhut* or more generally the side of holiness. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|1:54a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56b|56b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76b|76b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88b|88b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|169b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-198b|198b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-50a|2:50a]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125b|125b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|3:47a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-207a|207a]].

[^380]: **will not see death…** Namely, the Angel of Death. On seeing the Angel of Death just before departing the world, see BT *Avodah Zarah* [[Avodah Zarah 20#20b|20b]]; *Kallah Rabbati* 3:1; *Ḥibbut ha-Qever*, ed. Higger, 257–58; *Beit ha-Midrash*, 1:150; *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-126b|3:126b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 393. On the verse from Psalms, see *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.05 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Eikhah#^zh-91d|91d]] (*MhN, Eikhah*).

[^381]: **Twelve Months…** On the sentence of Hell as lasting twelve months, see M *Eduyyot* 2:10; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 17#17a|17a]]; *Seder Olam Rabbah* 3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-68b|1:68b]], [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-106b|106b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130b|130b]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245b|2:245b]] (*Heikh*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-25b|25b]] (*MhN*).

    Apparently, the “twelve months” on the side of holiness allude to the twelve camps of angels or supernal powers that accompany *Shekhinah*. See *Matoq mi-Devash* for a different interpretation.

    On the seven abodes of Hell, see BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 10#10b|10b]]; *Midrash Tehillim* 11:6; *Midrash Konen* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 2:30, 35); *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-40a|1:40a]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|62b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-237b|237b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-150b|2:150b]]; [[Zohar 9.06 - Parashat Qorah#^z3-178a|3:178a]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285b|285b–286a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-25b|25b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*); *Massekhet Geihinnom* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 1:149); Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:20, n. 56. See also *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 53.

[^382]: **First hall…** The counterpart of the first hall on the side of holiness, which corresponds to *Yesod* and *Malkhut*. Hence, this hall is an empty pit lacking support—as opposed to *Malkhut*, which is the well of living water and *Yesod*, the support of *Malkhut*. On the empty pit, see [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^37-24|Genesis 37:24]], describing the pit into which Joseph was thrown by his brothers. See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*). Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-185a|1:185a]].

[^383]: **Dumah… *Tohari’el*…** On Dumah’s receiving the soul rejected by *Tohari’el*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245a|2:245a]]–b (*Heikh*; above, page 59). On the angel *Tohari’el*, whose name derives from the root טהר (*thr*), “pure,” see *Sidrei de-Shimmusha Rabba* (ed. Scholem), 123; [Moses de León?], *Seder Gan Eden*, 268 (131); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 403; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-40a|1:40a]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108a|108a]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245a|2:245a]] (*Heikh*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-68c|68c]] (*ShS*). Cf. Schäfer, *Synopse zur Hekhalot-Literatur*, §427.

    דומה (*Dumah*), literally “silence,” is a name for the netherworld in the Bible. See [[Psalms 094#^94-17|Psalms 94:17]]: *Were not YHVH a help to me, my soul would have nearly dwelled in dumah*. In rabbinic literature, Dumah is the angel in charge of the souls of the dead (BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 18#18b|18b]], *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 152#152b|152b]], *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 5#5a|5a]], *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 94#94a|94a]]; *Tanḥuma, Ki Tissa* 33). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-8a|1:8a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|62b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-94a|94a]], [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-102a|102a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 11.08 - Tosefta#^z1-121a|121a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 10.07 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-123b|123b]] (labeled as *Tos*, but in fact *MhN*), 124a (*MhN*), 130b, 218b, 237b; 2:18a (*MhN*), 150b, 207a. Dumah also appears in the first abode of Hell in *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*). Dumah presides “above and below” because he is stationed here in the first hall of impurity, and because the third hall is named after him (see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264b|2:264b]]).

    “Wardens of judgment” renders נימוסין‎ גרדיני (*gardinei nimusin*). *Gardinei* derives from the Castilian *guardián*, “guardian.” See Corominas, *Diccionario*, 3:246–48.

[^384]: **פתותי (*Fatoti*)…** A person’s wandering eye is attributed to the activity of *Fatoti* and his minions, who—as his name suggests—seduces people to look upon inappropriate matters.

[^385]: **smashes its eyes… they belong to him!** The eyes that fell victim to *Fatoti*’s seduction belong to him. On *Fatoti* as agent, see *Bemidbar Rabbah* 17:6: “The heart and the eyes are the agents for the body.” See also Rashi on [[Torah 37 - Sh'lach#^15-39|Numbers 15:39]] (*You shall not stray after your heart and after your eyes*): “The heart and the eyes are the spies for the body. They are its agents for sinning; the eye sees, the heart covets, and the body commits the transgression.” See also *Bemidbar Rabbah* 10:2.

    On judgment or punishment of the grave, known as *ḥibbut ha-qever*, see 3 Enoch 28:10; *Ḥibbut ha-Qever*, ed. Higger, 258–61; *Beit ha-Midrash*, 1:150–52; 5:49; *Sefer Ḥasidim*, ed. Margaliot, par. 30; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-185a|1:185a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-225a|225a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151a|2:151a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-199b|199b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-211b|211b]]; [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-53a|3:53a]], [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-126b|126b]].

[^386]: **snakes and scorpions…** See *Targum Yerushalmi* on [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^37-24|Genesis 37:24]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 84:16; BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 22#22a|22a]]; *Tanḥuma, Vayeshev* 2; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79b|79b]] (*MhN, Rut*). Here, the “snakes and scorpions” signify forces of torment.

[^387]: **גמגימא (*Gamgima*)…** The chief spirit of the first hall of impurity. The verse from Job alludes to *Gamgima* cryptically via the word *gam*. Thus *Gamgima* works with Satan, the ultimate impure spirit of all. On the verse from Job, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-32b|2:32b]]; [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231a|3:231a]]. On the rejection of prayer in the first hall of holiness, see 2:245b (*Heikh*).

[^388]: **a filthy word that a person utters…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-249b|2:249b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 86). “To taint the holy site” means to impair *Shekhinah*.

    On the radical idea of weakening holy power or weakening God, see *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:33, in the name of Rabbi Yehudah son of Rabbi Simon: “When Israel enact the will of the Omnipresent, they strengthen heavenly power, as is said: *In God we generate strength* ([[Psalms 060#^60-14|Psalms 60:14]]). When Israel do not enact the will of the Omnipresent, they weaken, as it were, the great power above, as is written: *The Rock that bore you*, תשי (*teshi*), *you weakened* ([[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-18|Deuteronomy 32:18]]).” The word *teshi* derives from the root נשה (*nshh*), “to forget,” and the simple meaning of the verse in Deuteronomy is: *The Rock that bore you, you forgot*. However, according to this midrashic meaning, the word means *you weakened*, based on the root תשש (*tshsh*), “to be weak.” On this theme, see also *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 319; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 23:12; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 25:1; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57b|1:57b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-32b|2:32b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-64a|64a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-65b|65b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-155b|155b]].

[^389]: **objects that a person throws… in a fit of rage…** On the relation between anger and idolatry, see BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 105#105b|105b]], in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan son of Nuri: “One who tears his garments in anger or breaks his vessels in anger, or scatters his money in anger—regard him as an idolator.”

    See also *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 3; Maimonides, *Commentary on the Mishnah, Avot* 2:9; idem, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot De’ot* 2:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.027b|1:27b]] (*TZ*); 2:182a–b; 3:179a (*RM*), 234b (*RM*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-21a|21a]] (*MhN*); *TZ* 56, 89b; [Moses de León?], *Orḥot Ḥayyim*, 33; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1333–34. See also *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-43a|3:43a]].

    The name *Safsirita* is attested in Cremona and Mantua. Other witnesses read טפסריניא (*Tafsarinya*).

[^390]: **strayed after a foreign god and worshiped another god…** On the phrase “foreign god,” see [[Psalms 081#^81-10|Psalms 81:10]]: *There shall be no foreign god* בך (*vekha*), *among you*—which can be read hyperliterally: *There shall be no foreign god within you*. On the hyperliteral reading of the verse as referring to the evil impulse, see JT *Nedarim* 9:1, 41b; BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 105#105b|105b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-182a|2:182a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-243a|243a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-106a|3:106a]]–b; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-21a|21a]] (*MhN*). On “another god,” see [[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^34-14|Exodus 34:14]].

[^391]: **Second hall…** Just as the second hall of holiness is more concealed than the first, the second hall of impurity is darker than the first. Like its counterpart on the side of holiness, this hall also has three openings. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-246b|2:246b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 67). On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see 2:263a (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377). See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79c|79c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^392]: **fornicate with their hands…** Masturbation. On this idiom, see BT *Niddah* [[Niddah 13#13b|13b]] (“committing adultery by hand”). In the world of the *Zohar*, masturbation is a heinous sin. See BT *Niddah* [[Niddah 13#13a|13a]]–b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56b|1:56b–57a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-100b|100b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-188a|188a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-219b|219b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-214b|2:214b]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-90a|3:90a]], [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-158a|158a]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 353; idem, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 230; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1365–66.

    On not seeing the *Shekhinah*, see BT *Niddah* [[Niddah 13#13b|13b]], in the name of Rabbi Ammi: “Whoever brings himself into the grip of lustful fantasy is barred from the domain of the blessed Holy One.”

    “Spilling seed on the ground” refers to withdrawal, as exemplified by Onan in [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^38-9|Genesis 38:9]]: *Onan knew that the seed would not be his and so when he came to bed with his brother’s wife, he would waste* [*his seed*] *on the ground, so to give no seed to his brother*. See Onqelos, ad loc. In *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:5, this mode of sexual practice is described as “threshing within, winnowing without.” The precise meaning of “eject seed unnaturally” is not clear, though presumably it refers to some forbidden sexual practice.

[^393]: **Boiling Seminal Emission…** The phrase derives from BT *Gittin* [[Gittin 57#57a|57a]], where it describes Balaam’s eternal punishment. Here it is the name of the aforementioned minions of *Astirya*. The drops of semen invigorate the powers of the Other Side, who subjugate the “covenant”—the *sefirah Yesod*, the divine phallus—to the demonic realm.

[^394]: **discharging seed in animals…** On עריות (*arayot*), “forbidden sexual relations,” and the prohibition on bestiality, see Leviticus 18. In his gloss to this passage, Scholem specified: “Sodomy.”

[^395]: ***Cup of Staggering*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-246b|2:246b–247a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 68) in the description of the second hall of holiness. The wicked who had been sentenced to death drank the cup administered by the court through which their sins were absolved; as for the remainder of the wicked, they now imbibe from the Cup of Staggering.

    See BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 43#43a|43a]], in the name of Rav Ḥisda: “One who is being led out to execution is given a grain of frankincense in a cup of wine to benumb his senses, as is said: *Give strong drink to the hapless and wine to the embittered* ([[Proverbs 31#^31-6|Proverbs 31:6]]).” See also *Tanḥuma, Pequdei* 2; Radak on [[Isaiah 51#^51-17|Isaiah 51:17]].

    [[Isaiah 51#^51-17|Isaiah 51:17]] reads: *Rouse, rouse yourself! Arise, O Jerusalem—you who from YHVH’s hand have drunk the cup of His wrath, you who have drained to the dregs the qubba’at, bowl—the cup of staggering*. See also ibid., 51:22. The phrase “the bitter day” derives from [[Amos 8#^8-10|Amos 8:10]].

    All the witnesses read “prince called *Cup of Staggering*” though this would appear to be an error, for in the *Heikhalot* the halls’ “princes” are never otherwise given such a name. Following Margaliot and other printed editions, I have emended “prince” to “cup.”

[^396]: **three bitter drops ooze…** See BT *Avodah Zarah* [[Avodah Zarah 20#20b|20b]]: “In his [the Angel of Death] hand is a drawn sword with a bitter drop suspended. When the sick person sees him, he is frightened and opens his mouth. The angel flings the drop inside. From this drop he dies; from this drop he turns putrid; from this drop his face turns green.” On the motif of three drops (as opposed to one as in the Talmudic account), see the description of the Angel of Death in *Ḥibbut ha-Qever*, ed. Higger, 258: “His knife has drops: from one [the person] dies, from one he turns putrid, from one his face turns green.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-267a|2:267a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-126b|3:126b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 393. Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148b|1:148b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231b|3:231b]].

    On *Ḥatsats*, see [[Proverbs 20#^20-17|Proverbs 20:17]]: *Bread gained by fraud may be tasty to a man, but later his mouth will be filled with* חצץ (*ḥatsats*), *gravel*. On *Mar ha-mavet*, see [[I Samuel 15#^15-32|1 Samuel 15:32]]: *Samuel said, “Bring forth to me Agag king of Amalek!” Agag went to him with mincing steps, and he thought “Ah*, המות‎ מר (*mar ha-mavet*), *death’s bitterness, is turned away!”* On *Qubba’at*, “bowl, goblet,” see [[Isaiah 51#^51-17|Isaiah 51:17]] cited in previous note.

[^397]: **insert this holy covenant into another woman…** Namely, the covenantal sign of circumcision, into a non-Jewish woman.

    The reference to an “alien god” may derive from [[Malachi 2#^2-11|Malachi 2:11]]: *Judah has broken faith; an abhorrent act has been committed in Israel and in Jerusalem. For Judah has desecrated the sanctuary of YHVH, which He loves, and has married the daughter of an alien god*. See also [[Psalms 081#^81-10|Psalms 81:10]]: *There shall be no foreign god among you, and you shall not bow to an alien god*. In the *Zohar*, the prohibition against bowing down to another god includes a warning not to lie down with a foreign woman.

    See BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 19#19a|19a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|1:93a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-131b|131b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-189b|189b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-3b|2:3b]], [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-7a|7a]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-57b|57b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-87b|87b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|90a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-243a|243a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-13b|3:13b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-57b|57b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-90a|90a]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|142a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-266a|266a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-21a|21a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-78c|78c]] (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 212–13; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 51 (63).

    The *Zohar*’s concern with sexual purity reflects the promiscuity prevalent in the Jewish community in thirteenth-century Castile. See Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 51–54 (63–67); Baer, in *Zion* 2 (1937):31–33, 36–44; idem, *History*, 1:250–63; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1371–72; Assis, “Sexual Behavior in Medieval Hispano-Jewish Society.”

    On the images of the impure women subsequently defiling the spirit of the deceased, see BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 3#3b|3b]]: “Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥmani said in the name of Rabbi Yonatan: ‘Whoever performs one commandment in this world, it precedes him and goes before him to the world to come…. Whoever commits one transgression in this world, it clings to him and goes before him to the Day of Judgment….’ Rabbi El’azar says, ‘It attaches itself to him like a dog, as is said: *he* [Joseph] *would not heed her to lie by her, to be with her* ([[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^39-10|Genesis 39:10]]). *To lie by her*—in this world; *to be with her*—in the world to come.’” See also *Tanḥuma, Vayeshev* 8.

    On *Sangadi’el*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248a|2:248a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264b|264b]] (both *Heikh*). The reading *Sangadi’el* is found in *Or Yaqar*, Cremona, and Mantua. Other witnesses read סנגריאל (*Sangri’el*), [cf. Spanish *sang real*, “royal blood,” and *san greal*, “Holy Grail”], on whom see Yakov Zvi Yules, *Sefer Qehilat Ya’akov*, entry סנ.

[^398]: **semen that he discharged into animals…** On Balaam’s bestial sorcery practices, see BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 105#105a|105a]]–b; *Avodah Zarah* [[Avodah Zarah 4#4b|4b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125b|1:125b–126a]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-207a|3:207a]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 14–15 (18); idem, *She’elot u-Tshuvot*, 75. On his punishment in boiling semen, see BT *Gittin* [[Gittin 57#57a|57a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-194b|3:194b]].

[^399]: **false material** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 55#55a|55a]], in the name of Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai: “Just as there cannot be wheat without straw, so there cannot be a dream without nonsense.” According to BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 55#55b|55b]], angels convey prophetic dreams, while demons convey false ones. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|1:83a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130a|130a]]–b, [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-183a|183a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-199b|199b–200a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-238a|238a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|2:130a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-195b|195b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248a|248a]] (*Heikh*), [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-267a|267a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25a|3:25a]], [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-156b|156b]].

[^400]: **were he not to convey a truthful matter…** Like the deceiver who combines truth with falsehood, so the demonic forces of this hall mingle their deceptions with words emanating from the holy side. See BT *Sotah* [[Sotah 35#35a|35a]]; Rashi on [[Torah 37 - Sh'lach#^13-27|Numbers 13:27]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-2b|1:2b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-215b|2:215b]]; [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-161a|3:161a]].

[^401]: **titillate human beings…** On demonic seduction, succubi, and incubi, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19b|1:19b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54b|54b–55a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|169b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|2:130a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-76b|3:76b–77a]]; Trachtenberg, *Jewish Magic and Superstition*, 51–54; Scholem, *On the Kabbalah*, 154–57; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1366–67. Cf. BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 151#151b|151b]], in the name of Rabbi Ḥanina: “One may not sleep alone in a house; and whoever sleeps alone in a house is seized by Lilith.” Cf. *Tanḥuma*, (Buber), *Bereshit* 17. See also BT *Ketubbot* [[Ketubot 46#46a|46a]]. On spirits that are “sometimes male, sometimes female,” see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-44a|1:44a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 43 and n. 96).

    Later printed editions append a clarifying statement to this paragraph: “Likewise, to women they appear as men.”

[^402]: ***strange woman*…** The demonic Other Side. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-38b|1:38b]] (*Heikh*).

[^403]: **Third hall…** Just as the third hall on the side of holiness is more radiant than all the preceding and contains four openings (see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248a|2:248a]]; above, page 77), so this hall is gloomier than the preceding halls of impurity and also contains four openings.

    On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-263a|2:263a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377) Dumah, literally “Silence,” designates the netherworld in the Bible. See [[Psalms 094#^94-17|Psalms 94:17]]: *Were not YHVH a help to me, my soul would have nearly dwelt in dumah*. Cf. [[Psalms 115#^115-17|Psalms 115:17]]. In rabbinic literature Dumah is the angel in charge of souls of the dead (BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 18#18b|18b]], *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 152#152b|152b]], *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 94#94a|94a]]). In the *Zohar* he retains this role and oversees Hell. On Dumah as a name of Hell, see *Midrash Tehillim* 11:6. See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79c|79c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^404]: **takes up weapons and stores them…** See BT *Bava Qamma* [[Bava Kamma 60#60b|60b]]: “Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? Keep your feet indoors.’… Our Rabbis taught: ‘A plague in town? One should not walk in the middle of the road because the Angel of Death walks there, for as soon as permission has been granted him, he strides brazenly.’… Our Rabbis taught: A plague in the town? One should not go alone to the synagogue because the Angel of Death stores his tools there.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-63a|1:63a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-101b|101b–102a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-107b|107b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182b|182b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204b|204b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-54a|3:54a]]–b; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-81c|81c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

    On the dangers of walking alone, see also M *Avot* 3:4; JT *Shabbat* 2:6, 5b; *Tanḥuma, Vayishlaḥ* 8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|1:69b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|169b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-205a|2:205a]]. See also BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 43#43b|43b]]: “To one person, [a demon] shows himself and causes harm; to two, he shows himself but does not cause harm; to three, he does not show himself at all.”

[^405]: ***Sangadi’el*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248a|2:248a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 78). Some witnesses read הנגדיאל (*Hangadi’el*).

[^406]: ***Anagriyon*…** Cf. BT *Pesaḥim* [[Pesachim 112#112a|112a]], where the word אניגרון (*Anigron*), perhaps designating the name of a demon, appears in an incantation against thirst. On “trembling fever” and “fire in the bones,” see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 32#32a|32a]]; *Gittin* [[Gittin 70#70a|70a]]; *Kallah Rabbati* 1:18.

[^407]: **Diphtheria, presiding over the killing of children…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248b|2:248b]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 80 and n. 76). On Lilith (who is associated or identified with Diphtheria) as variously playing and toying with, or laughing at victims, see also 2:96b, 111a; 3:77a, 234a.

    Presumably, the awkward phrase “the woman of the child” means “mother of the child,” which is the wording found only in later editions. In his gloss on “mother of the child,” Scholem suggests that it alludes to the Arabic name for *Askara*, “*umm al-sibyan*,” literally “mother of lads,” a term referring to the female demon responsible for infant death.

[^408]: ***Agirison*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248b|2:248b–249a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 81 and nn. 78 and 79).

[^409]: **Wrath and Rage…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-249b|2:249b]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 85 and n. 95).

    The phrase “but do not trust him and are jealous of him” is a conjectural emendation. The manuscripts and first printed editions seem garbled here; they read: “but they trust him and are not jealous of him.” Cf. Margaliot, *Matoq mi-Devash, Sullam*, Edri.

[^410]: **that they will be saddened and find no delight therefrom…** Wrath and Rage and their minions act to deprive one of the joy of fulfilling a commandment. See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 31#31a|31a]]: “One should not stand to pray while immersed in sorrow or laziness or laughter or chatter or frivolity or idle talk, but only in the joy of [fulfilling] a commandment.”

    The verse from Deuteronomy continues: *with which YHVH was furious with you to destroy you*.

[^411]: **evil tongue… gossip…** Both of these render בישא‎ לישנא (*lishana bisha*), “evil tongue; evil speech; gossip, slander.” On evil speech as arousing the demonic realm, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-46b|3:46b–47a]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-53a|53a]]. Cf. [[Torah 39 - Chukat#^21-4|Numbers 21:4–6]]; *Tanḥuma, Ḥuqqat* 19; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 19:22. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265b|2:265b]] (*Heikh*; below, page 194).

    On the general kabbalistic principle “arousal above depends on arousal below,” see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-156b|156b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]]–b, [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-233a|233a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-235a|235a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-35b|35b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-47b|47b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125a|125a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-256b|256b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-31b|3:31b]], [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-40b|40b]], [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|47a]], [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47b|47b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|92a–b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-105a|105a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]], [[Zohar 8.06 - Parashat Be-Huqqotai#^z3-112b|112b]], [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-145a|145a]] (*Piq*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 144.

[^412]: ***writhing snake, elusive snake*…** Signifying the demonic realm. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266a|2:266a]] (*Heikh*; below, page 195). Cf. 2:35a–b, 244b. The “scales” of the snake (and its “coat” of skin) are the forces through which it is active in the world.

    The full verse in Isaiah reads: *On that day YHVH will punish—with His fierce, great, mighty sword—Leviathan the elusive snake, Leviathan the writhing snake, and He will slay the dragon in the sea*. See also [[Job 26#^26-12|Job 26:12–13]].

[^413]: **Even though there is a fixed time…** According to *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 14, snakes shed their skin once every seven years. See also ibid., 34: “The voices of five [objects of creation] go from one end of the world to the other, and their voices are inaudible…. When the serpent sheds its skin, its voice goes from one end of the world to the other, and its voice is not heard.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-168b|3:168b]].

    On the separation of the male snake from his partner, cf. BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 74#74b|74b]]: “*God created the great sea serpents* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-21|Genesis 1:21]])…. Rabbi Yoḥanan said, ‘This is Leviathan, the elusive snake, and Leviathan the writhing snake, as is said: *On that day YHVH will punish—with His fierce*, [*great, mighty*] *sword*—[*Leviathan the elusive snake, Leviathan the writhing snake*] ([[Isaiah 27#^27-1|Isaiah 27:1]]).’ Rav Yehudah said in the name of Rav, ‘Everything that the blessed Holy One created in His world, He created male and female. Even *Leviathan the elusive snake* and *Leviathan the writhing snake* He created male and female, and if they mated with one another, they would destroy the entire world. What did the blessed Holy One do? He castrated the male and killed the female, salting her for the righteous in the world to come, as is written: *He will slay the serpent of the sea* (ibid.).’” On Leviathan and his mate, see *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-21|Genesis 1:21]]; Isaac ben Jacob ha-Kohen, *Ma’amar al ha-Atsilut ha-Semalit*, 262–63; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34b|1:34b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|46b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-34a|2:34a]]–b, [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-108b|108b]], [[Zohar 5.06 - Sifra di-Tsni'uta#^z2-176b|176b]] (*SdTs*), [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244b|244b]]; Idel, “Livyatan u-Vat Zugo.”

[^414]: **site called Pit…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-263a|2:263a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 182 and n. 386). On the serpent’s cry, see *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 34, cited in the previous note.

[^415]: **holy tongue… Tongue of Holiness…** As “evil tongue” (gossip) stimulates the demonic forces, so words of Torah empower angelic potencies known as “holy tongue,” who in turn stimulate *Malkhut*, who is described here as “Tongue of Holiness,” insofar as She gives expression to “holiness above”—namely *Ḥokhmah*. On Tongue of Holiness as *Malkhut* and *Ḥokhmah*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-61a|3:61a]]. On Hebrew as Holy Tongue, see Naḥmanides on [[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^30-13|Exodus 30:13]].

    “Holiness above and holiness below” may indicate *Binah* and *Malkhut* respectively.

[^416]: **first waters, upper waters… lower waters, called final waters…** See BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 53#53b|53b]]: “*Sanctify yourselves*—these are the first waters; *and be holy*—these are the final waters; *for holy* (ibid.)—this is oil; *am I, YHVH your God* ([[Torah 30 - Kedoshim#^20-7|Leviticus 20:7]])—this is blessing.”

    “First waters” refer to the laving of hands before eating; “final waters” to washing one’s hands with “fingerbowl water” after the meal.

    Here, first waters are associated with “upper waters,” the flow of bounty originating in *Binah* through the divine masculine below into *Malkhut*, whereas final waters are associated with “lower waters,” the arousal of the divine feminine to the masculine. Food is “in the middle” insofar as the meal comes between both waters, though it is understood here more symbolically as designating the fruit of union between the masculine and feminine. Ultimately, “food” derives from the upper waters—from the upper reaches of the sefirotic realm.

    On “final waters” (or “fingerbowl water”), see also *Tosefta Berakhot* (Lieberman) 5:13; BT *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 17#17b|17b]], *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 105#105a|105a]]–106a; *Tanḥuma Balaq* 15; *Tanḥuma Balaq* (Buber) 24; Rashi, s.v. *she-melaḥ sedomit yesh* and Tosafot, s.v. *mayyim aḥaronim ḥovah* on *Eruvin* [[Eruvin 17#17b|17b]]; idem on *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 105#105a|105a]], s.v. *mayyim rishonim*; Maimonides, *Hilkhot Berakhot* 6:3; 7:12; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-154b|2:154b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-169a|169a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266b|266b]] (*Heikh*; below, page 200); 3:186b, 246a (*RM*), 273b (*RM*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-86d|86d]], [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-87b|87b]]–c (*MhN, Rut*); Joseph of Hamadan, *Sefer Ta’amei ha-Mitsvot*, 87; Baḥya ben Asher, *Shulḥan Shel Arba*, 463, 475–6; Jacob ben Asher, *Tur, Oraḥ Ḥayyim* 181; Joseph Caro, *Shulḥan Arukh* 181:1; Ta-Shma, *Ha-Nigleh she-ba-Nistar*, 62, 80–87.

    The phrase “holy ones of the Most High” derives from [[Daniel 07#^7-18|Daniel 7:18–27]], where it refers to Israel. Here it designates the kabbalists.

[^417]: **Fourth hall…** The fourth hall of holiness is known as זכות (*Zekhut*), “Merit,” which also means “innocence” in a forensic context. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-251a|2:251a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 94); 1:43b (*Heikh*). Its counterpart is therefore the Hall of Guilt. The Hall of Guilt also appears in 2:6a.

    On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see 2:263a (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377). See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79c|79c]] (*MhN, Rut*). On Miry Clay, see [[Psalms 040#^40-3|Psalms 40:3]].

[^418]: **they are weighed together…** If merits outweigh sins, the side of holiness prevails; conversely, if sins outweigh merits, the Other Side prevails.

    On weighing sins and merits, see Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Teshuvah* 3:1–4, and see next note. Cf. BT *Qiddushin* [[Kiddushin 40#40a|40a]]–b. On the verse from Ecclesiastes, see BT *Ḥagigah* [[Chagigah 15#15a|15a]]; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 7:14. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252a|2:252a]] (*Heikh*).

[^419]: **inscribed for either life or death…** See BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 16#16b|16b]], in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan: “Three books are opened on Rosh Hashanah: one for the completely wicked, one for the completely righteous, and one for those between. The completely righteous are immediately inscribed and sealed for life. The completely wicked are immediately inscribed and sealed for death. Those in between remain pending from Rosh Hashanah until Yom Kippur. If they prove worthy, they are inscribed for life; if not, for death.” See also *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33b|2:33b]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-100b|3:100b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.10 - Merkevet Yehezqel#^zh-20c|20c]].

[^420]: **other gods…** Demonic forces. “Other gods” appears in [[Torah 17 - Yitro#^20-3|Exodus 20:3]] and frequently.

[^421]: **אל (*el*), god, corresponding to the other one in the side of holiness…** Namely, the spirit *Zekhut’el* in the fourth hall of holiness. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-251a|2:251a]]–b (*Heikh*; above, page 94).

    *Alien god* derives from [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-12|Deuteronomy 32:12]]; [[Malachi 2#^2-11|Malachi 2:11]]; [[Psalms 081#^81-10|Psalms 81:10]].

[^422]: **Boiling צואה (*Tso’ah*), Excrement, as is written: ‘צא (*Tse*), *Out!’ you will call to it*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252a|2:252a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 98 and n. 139) in the description of the fourth hall of holiness. Here, the phrase from Isaiah is construed as ‘*Out!’ you will call to him*, i.e., “Get out of the study house!”

[^423]: **Scaly Plague… defile all those masters of evil tongue…** “Scaly Plague” renders צרעת‎ נגע (*nega tsara’at*); alternatively, “scaly affliction.” On the term *tsara’at*, often translated imprecisely as *leprosy*, see Milgrom, *Leviticus*, 1:774–76, 816–26.

    See [[Torah 27 - Tazria#^13-2|Leviticus 13:2–3]]: *If a person has on the skin of his body an inflammation or a rash or a shiny spot and it becomes a scaly affliction on the skin of his body, he shall be brought* [or: *it shall be reported*] *to Aaron the priest or to one of his sons the priests. The priest shall examine the affliction on the skin of his body, and if the hair in the affliction has turned white and the affliction seems deeper than the skin of his body, it is scaly affliction; when the priest sees it, he shall declare him impure*.

    On evil speech as causing scaly affliction, see [[Torah 36 - Beha'alotcha#^12-10|Numbers 12:10]]; *Tosefta Nega’im* 6:7; *Sifra, Metsora* 5:7, 73a; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 275; *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^24-9|Deuteronomy 24:9]]; *Mishnat Rabbi Eli’ezer*, 9, pp. 172–75; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 9; B, 16; JT *Sotah* 2:1, 17d; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 16:1–7; 17:3; 18:4; BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 97#97a|97a]], *Arakhin* [[Arakhin 15#15b|15b]]–16a; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 5:5; *Devarim Rabbah* 6:8, 10; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 23, *Metsora* 1–2, 4; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Shemot* 20, *Metsora* 1–7, 10; *Midrash Tehillim* 52:1; *Shemot Rabbah* 3:13; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 7:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-122a|2:122a]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-46b|3:46b–47a]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-53a|53a]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-183b|183b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-206a|206a]]–b. Many of these sources interpret המצורע (*ha-metsora*), *the one afflicted with scales*, according to the method of *notariqon* (shorthand) as רע‎ המוציא (*ha-motsi ra*), “the one who utters evil,” or רע‎ שם‎ המוציא (*ha-motsi shem ra*), “the one who defames a person.”

    On *Nega*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|2:252b]] (*Heikh*) in the description of the fourth hall of holiness. On “evil tongue,” see 2:264b–265a (*Heikh*; above, page 189) in the description of the third hall of impurity.

[^424]: ***Nega* is appointed over all tables…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|2:252b]] (*Heikh*; above, pp. 102–3 and nn. 148 and 150) in the description of the fourth hall of holiness.

[^425]: **[holiday] meals follow this pattern** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-94a|3:94a]].

[^426]: **children, life, and sustenance inversely…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-252b|2:252b]] (*Heikh*; above, pp. 103–4 and n. 153) in the description of the fourth hall of holiness.

[^427]: **אריריא (*Arirya*)…** The name is connected here with the root ארר (*'rr*), “to curse; damn.” Curses are seized by demonic forces that activate Leviathan to arouse curses of his own.

    On the danger of cursing oneself, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-14b|1:14b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-155b|3:155b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-246a|246a]] (*RM*). Cf. M *Shevu’ot* 4:13; BT *Shevu’ot* 36a. On the demonic effects of wrong or evil speech, see BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 19#19a|19a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-175a|1:175a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-195b|195b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-47b|2:47b]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-46b|3:46b]], [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|47a]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-53a|53a]], [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-85a|85a]], [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-155b|155b]]. On arousing *the writhing snake*, see 2:265a (*Heikh*; above, page 190).

    In chapter 3, Job curses the day of his birth. Verse 8 reads: *Let the day-cursers damn it; those skilled to stir Leviathan*. The *Zohar* connects לווטין (*levvatin*), “curses,” with the similar-sounding לויתן (*livyatan*), “Leviathan.”

[^428]: **Job cursed himself…** Job’s curse arouses the day-cursers. On the Day-Cursers as presiding over moments and hours, cf. the description of Balaam in BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 7#7a|7a]], where his sorcerous power is described as knowing how to tap the one moment of divine anger each day.

    In place of ומתוקפוי (*u-me-toqpoi*), “and from its power,” some witnesses read ומתסקופי (*u-me-tasquppei*), “and from [its] ploys” or “from false accusations.” See *Targum Onqelos*, [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^22-14|Deuteronomy 22:14]]; Radak on [[Judges 14#^14-4|Judges 14:4]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|1:169b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-179b|179b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-65a|2:65a]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-172b|3:172b]], [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-266b|266b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-18d|18d]] (*MhN*).

[^429]: **Fifth hall…** Whereas the fifth hall of holiness is called אהבה (*Ahavah*), “Love,” its counterpart on the side of impurity is known as איבה (*Eivah*), “Enmity.” Like the Hall of Love, this hall also has one opening and one prince above it. However, whereas the prince in the Hall of Love acts as Israel’s advocate, the prince here acts as accuser. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253a|2:253a]] (*Heikh*; above, pages 104 and 105).

    On the mystery of the foreskin, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244b|2:244b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-258a|258a]] (*Heikh*; above, pages 139 and 141). On foreskin and the demonic realm, see also 1:13a, 18a, 35b, 91b, 103b; 3:44a–b (*Piq*); Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (68); idem, *Mishkan ha-Edut*, 13a. On *Eivah*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-111a|2:111a]] (standard printed editions).

    On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see above, *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-263a|2:263a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377). See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79c|79c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

    The verse from Genesis continues: *and between your seed and her seed. He will strike you at the head, and you will strike him at the heel*. This constitutes the conclusion of the divine curse delivered to the serpent.

[^430]: **שודד (*Shoded*)…** Meaning “bandit, robber.” The name of the spirit שודד (*Shoded*) might be interpreted as the counterpart of the “breasts” [Heb. singular שד (*shad*), דד (*dad*)] of the fifth hall of holiness. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253a|2:253a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 107).

    The phrase *wrack and ruin* appears in [[Isaiah 59#^59-7|Isaiah 59:7]], 60:18; [[Jeremiah 48#^48-3|Jeremiah 48:3]]; cf. [[Isaiah 51#^51-19|Isaiah 51:19]].

    On the connection between Sheol and pillaging in mountains, see *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 43 and Luria’s note 44. On *the flame of the ever-turning sword*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-44a|1:44a]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253a|2:253a]] (both *Heikh*) in the description of the fifth hall of holiness, where the phrase designates forces of judgment. See also *Bereshit Rabbah* 21:9: “*Ever-turning*—changing: sometimes male, sometimes female; sometimes spirits, sometimes angels.” See also BT *Yoma* [[Yoma 75#75a|75a]], where demons are said to “turn many colors.”

[^431]: ***You will laugh at plunder and famine*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253b|2:253b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 108) in the description of the fifth hall of holiness.

[^432]: **driving these two spirits away…** The significance of the verse from Job is now apparent. The virtuous have the capacity to banish these two spirits; hence they can laugh at them.

    On the charitable practices of the nations who perform kindness only that they might be made strong—and so subjugate Israel—see BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 10#10b|10b]]: “Rabban Yoḥanan son of Zakkai said to his disciples, ‘My sons, what is the meaning of the verse: *Charity exalts a nation, but kindness is a sin for the peoples* ([[Proverbs 14#^14-34|Proverbs 14:34]])?’ Rabbi Eli’ezer answered and said, ‘*Charity exalts a nation*—Israel, as is written: *Who is like your people Israel a unique nation on earth*? ([[I Chronicles 17#^17-21|1 Chronicles 17:21]]). *But kindness is a sin for the peoples*—all the charity and kindness performed by the peoples of the world [i.e., the heathens] is counted to them as sin, because they do it only to magnify themselves, as is said: *That they may offer pleasing sacrifices to the God of heaven, and pray for the life of the king and of his sons* ([[Ezra 06#^6-10|Ezra 6:10]])….’ Rabbi Eli’ezer ha-Moda’i says, ‘… All the charity and kindness performed by the peoples of the world is counted to them as sin, for they do it only to vilify us….’” See also *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 2:5; *Tanḥuma, Ki Tissa* 5.

[^433]: ***they made me guardian of the vineyards*…** The verse is interpreted as being spoken by *Shekhinah*, who laments the fact that through Israel’s disdain for kindness, and the other nations’ practice of kindness, She nourishes the nations (*vineyards*) rather than Israel (*my own vineyard*). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-45b|3:45b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-59b|59b]], [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-119b|119b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-209b|209b]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-69d|69d]] (*ShS*).

[^434]: **Foreskin, Clippings of Foreskin, Branches of Foreskin…** People who delay the circumcision of their sons, or who do not wait three years before eating new fruit (“the years of foreskin”), are subject to various demonic forces—all called Foreskin. Zipporah acted to avert this demonic danger when she circumcised her son.

    See [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^4-24|Exodus 4:24–26]]: *At an encampment on the way, YHVH encountered him and sought to kill him. Zipporah took a flint and cut off her son’s foreskin and touched it to his feet, and she said, “Yes, a bridegroom of blood you are to me.” He let him go. Then did she say, “A bridegroom of blood by the circumcising.”* Commentators are divided over the identity of the intended victim: Moses, or his son.

    On this extraordinary biblical narrative, see *Mekhilta, Amaleq* (*Yitro*) 1; *Targum Yerushalmi*, ad loc.; *Shemot Rabbah* 5:8; BT *Nedarim* [[Nedarim 31#31b|31b]]–32a; JT *Nedarim* 3:14, 38b; *Midrash Aggadah*, [[Torah 13 - Shemot#^4-24|Exodus 4:24]]; Rashi, Ibn Ezra, and Baḥya, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93b|1:93b]]; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, ad loc., nn. 137, 143.

    See BT *Nedarim* [[Nedarim 32#32a|32a]], in the name of Rabbi Yehudah son of Biznah: “When Moses our Master neglected circumcision, Wrath and Rage came and swallowed him up, leaving only his feet [a euphemism for phallus]. Immediately, *Zipporah took a flint and cut off her son’s foreskin*. Immediately, *He let him go*.”

    The term ערלה (*orlah*), “foreskin,” also pertains to fruit. According to [[Torah 30 - Kedoshim#^19-23|Leviticus 19:23]], a tree’s fruit must not be eaten during the first three years of growth: *When you come to the land and plant any fruit-bearing tree, you shall treat its foreskin with its fruit as foreskin* [or: *you shall leave its fruits uncircumcised*]. *Three years it shall be foreskin* [or: *uncircumcised, forbidden*] *to you; it shall not be eaten*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244b|2:244b]].

    *Gezar Dinnaya* means “sentence, verdict, decree.”

[^435]: **Sheol and Avadon…** In the Bible, שאול (*she’ol*) is the underworld, abode of the dead—sometimes paired with אבדון (*avaddon*), “destruction, ruin,” which may refer to a distinct area of the underworld reserved for the wicked. According to rabbinic tradition, there are seven divisions of Hell, two of which are Sheol and Avadon (see above, note 377). On Avadon, see [[Proverbs 15#^15-11|Proverbs 15:11]]; [[Job 26#^26-6|Job 26:6]]; and [[Revelation 09#^9-11|Revelation 9:11]], where Avadon is the name of *the angel of the bottomless pit*.

[^436]: **for this entire hall** is enmity See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-266a|2:266a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 195). The spirit injecting hostility is the counterpart of the various forces in the fifth hall of holiness that “convey love between Israel below and the blessed Holy One above” (see 2:253b; above, page 108). The spirit is named below.

[^437]: ***two-edged sword*** Alluding to the two worlds in which the seduced die: this world, and the world to come. See *Midrash Mishlei* 5:4. Proverbs 5 warns against the wiles of the adulteress, here symbolizing the demonic feminine. Verse 3 reads: *For the lips of a strange woman drip honey; her palate is smoother than oil*.

    On [[Proverbs 05#^5-4|Proverbs 5:4]], see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-267a|2:267a]] (*Heikh*; below, page 202) in the description of the sixth hall of impurity. See also 2:244a.

[^438]: **אפרא (*Afra*)… ash of ashen cinders…** Standard print editions read אפרירא (*Afrira*). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-9b|1:9b]], which mentions *Afrira*, though there the name is spelled עפרירא (with an *ayin* rather than an *alef*). Here the name *Afra* (with an *alef*) derives from אפר (*efer*), “ash,” rather than עפר (*afar*), “dust,” though the *Zohar* readily connects the two terms. (So already [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-27|Genesis 18:27]]; [[Job 30#^30-19|Job 30:19]]; [[Job 42#^42-6|42:6]].) Unlike fruitful *dust of gold*, signifying *Malkhut, Afra* is barren.

    The verse from Numbers describes the purification ritual required by a person (or object) that has come into contact with the dead. The dust from the purification offering was mixed with water and then sprinkled on the person (or object) with hyssop. According to the *Zohar*, the verse contains two secrets: firstly, the spirit *Afra* is included in this *ḥattat*, “purification offering,” which pertains to the primordial snake—it is the dust of the offering; and secondly, one who performs *ḥattat*, “a sin,” energizes the demonic dust. See [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^4-7|Genesis 4:7]]: *At the opening ḥattat, sin, crouches*.

    On *Malkhut* as dust, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49a|1:49a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-170a|170a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-249b|249b–250a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-23b|2:23b–24a]]; [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-34b|3:34b]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 344–45; idem, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 171; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 57–58, 62, 93–96 (70–71, 77–78, 118–22). On *dust of gold*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-249b|1:249b–250a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-24a|2:24a]]; [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-34b|3:34b]]; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 344.

    The context in Job 28 is the process of mining metals; the full verse reads: *A place whose stones are sapphire and that has dust of gold*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-170a|1:170a]], where ash symbolizes Lilith, the demonic feminine: “Ash, residue of fire, never generating fruit; dust, yielding all fruit, totality of above and below.” See also 1:80a (*ST*); 2:254b (*Heikh*; above, page 116).

[^439]: **This [spirit] is included in *the bitter curse-conveying waters*…** Just as the spirit *Afra* is included in the purification offering, so too is it included in the water imbibed by the suspected adulteress.

    [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^5-11|Numbers 5:11–31]] contains the law relating to a wife suspected of adultery. In such a case, the suspicious husband brings his wife to the sanctuary [Dwelling or Tabernacle], where she undergoes a trial by ordeal. The priest makes her drink a potion consisting of sacred water to which have been added dust from the sanctuary floor and a parchment containing a curse (whose written letters dissolve in the water). The curse spells out the consequences: if she is guilty, her genital area will distend and she will be unable to conceive. If, however, the water has no effect on her, she is declared innocent and she will be blessed with seed. See Milgrom, *Numbers*, 37.

    According to the *Zohar*’s reading here, whereas the Dwelling symbolizes *Malkhut*, “floor” signifies the realm of the halls beneath. The *dust that is on the floor of the Dwelling* ([[Torah 35 - Nasso#^5-17|Numbers 5:17]]) thus implies the demonic forces beneath *Malkhut*. On the significance of that dust, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-125a|3:125a]]. Cf. 2:6a, where the *bitter curse-conveying waters* are located in the Hall of Guilt. See also 1:80a (*ST*).

    The *bitter curse-conveying waters* located in the fifth hall of impurity are the counterpart of the *many waters* ([[Song of Songs 8#^8-7|Song of Songs 8:7]]) in the fifth hall of holiness. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253b|2:253b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 109). The phrase *woman of whoredom* derives from [[Hosea 01#^1-2|Hosea 1:2]].

[^440]: **final waters are חובה (*ḥovah*), an obligation…** See BT *Ḥullin* [[Chullin 105#105a|105a]], in the name of Rabbi Yitsḥak son of Ashyan: “The first waters [i.e., washing the hands before the meal] are מצוה (*mitsvah*), a meritorious act; and the last [waters, i.e., washing after the meal] are חובה (*ḥovah*), an obligation.”

    In the continuation of the Talmudic discussion (105b), Abbaye notes, “At first I thought the reason why the last washing may not be performed over the ground was that it made a mess; but now my Master has told me: It is because an evil spirit rests upon it.”

    Here, the *Zohar* plays with Hebrew *ḥovah*, “obligation,” and Aramaic *ḥovah*, “sin, guilt.” This dirty water is offered to the sinful Other Side to satisfy and appease him—and prevent him from dominating.

    On first waters and final waters, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|2:265a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 191 and n. 416) in the description of the third hall of impurity. See especially 2:154b; 3:186b; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-87b|87b]]–c (*MhN, Rut*). On assuaging the Other Side, see above, note 222.

    “Supernal pure waters” indicates the flow from *Binah*, corresponding to “first” or “upper” waters—as opposed to “final” or “lower” waters, which stem from *Malkhut*.

[^441]: **Sixth hall…** Just as the sixth hall of holiness is distinct from the preceding halls and marks the site where unification begins, so this hall is supreme above all. On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-263a|2:263a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377). See also *ZḤ* [[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-79c|79c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^442]: **a conjoined house…** The verse from Proverbs alludes to the unification of the four openings of the hall. The full verse reads: *Better to live on a corner of the roof than a contentious woman in a shared* [or: *spacious*] *house*. See also [[Proverbs 25#^25-24|Proverbs 25:24]].

    On the four openings (and directions) of the sixth hall of holiness, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253b|2:253b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 110).

[^443]: **wicked kisses…** Whereas the sixth hall of holiness contains the mystery of “kisses” and “lips,” the corresponding hall of impurity contains “wicked kisses” and the *lips of a strange woman*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-253b|2:253b–254a]] (*Heikh*). See also 2:258b (*Heikh*).

[^444]: ***Do not lust for her beauty*…** The verse from Proverbs describes *the woman of evil*, which in the *Zohar* personifies the demonic realm.

[^445]: **Here hang all worldly desires…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-258b|2:258b]] (*Heikh*; above, page 144). Like the *woman of evil* in the preceding paragraph, the woman *decked out like a harlot* also personifies the demonic realm. Cf. 1:148a–b (*ST*). The encounter with the harlot described in Proverbs 7 is expounded for the remainder of the description of this sixth hall of impurity. As the sixth hall of holiness (corresponding to the *sefirah Tif’eret*) is the totality of the halls beneath it, so too this hall.

[^446]: **descends and seduces, ascends and accuses…** The demonic harlot “abides-not-abiding” insofar as she descends to seduce and then ascends to accuse. Compare Satan’s itinerary in BT *Bava Batra* [[Bava Batra 16#16a|16a]]: “He descends and seduces, ascends and arouses wrath, obtains authorization and seizes the soul.” See Rashi on BT *Shabbat* [[Shabbat 89#89a|89a]], s.v. *ba ha-satan*; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-10b|1:10b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|46b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125a|125a]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|148a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-190a|190a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33b|2:33b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|196a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-268b|268b]] (*Heikh*).

[^447]: **sword in hand, three drops within…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264a|2:264a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 185 and n. 396) in the description of the second hall of impurity. On the demonic harlot’s kisses and transformation into a body of fire, cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|1:148a]] (*ST*).

    [[Proverbs 05#^5-3|Proverbs 5:3]] reads in full: *For the lips of a strange woman drip honey, and her palate is smoother than oil*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-203b|2:203b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-244a|244a]]. On [[Proverbs 05#^5-4|Proverbs 5:4]], see 2:266b (*Heikh*; above, page 198) in the description of the fifth hall of impurity.

[^448]: **he flings it into the person’s mouth…** In this paragraph the pronoun “he” refers to the Angel of Death, who is the harlot transformed. On the drops penetrating the gut, cf. *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 16: “… the evil impulse in his intestines”; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-52a|1:52a]].

[^449]: **soul departs with great difficulty…** One who cleaves to the Other Side during life endures a more difficult and painful death.

    “With great difficulty” renders בפיטורי (*be-fiturei*), an ambiguous word deriving from an ambiguous phrase in BT *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 8#8a|8a]], where ושת‎ בפי‎ כפיטורי (*ke-fiturei be-fi veset*), “like the gushing water at the entrance of a canal,” describes death by diphtheria. Some understand the phrase to denote ropes being pulled through loop-holes in the boards of a ship. See Rashi, ad loc., s.v. *ke-fiturei be-fi veset*. Whatever the precise meaning, the phrase describes the pangs of a painful death. In the Talmudic passage, such painful death is contrasted with death by divine kiss. See Jastrow, 1161. See also BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 28b–29a; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Miqqets* 15. See also *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-256a|2:256a]] (*Heikh*; above, page 128) in the description of the sixth hall of holiness.

[^450]: **grooming and curling** Vanity is associated with the demonic realm. Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 22:6, in the name of Rabbi Ammi: “The evil impulse does not walk on the side, but in the middle of the street; and when he sees a person painting his eyes, smoothing his hair, swinging his heel, he says, ‘This one is mine!’” See also *Bereshit Rabbah* 87:3; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-189b|1:189b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-190b|190b]].

[^451]: **another prince, which that other prince causes a person to arouse…** Apparently meaning that the activities of *Seqatofa* (which rouse a person to vanity) in turn arouse another demonic force, *Asirta*. The impulse to look upon oneself in the mirror also hails from the Other Side.

    On *Asirta*, cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-234b|3:234b]]. On dreams and their deceptions, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-264a|2:264a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 186 and n. 399) in the description of the second hall of impurity.

[^452]: **Lilith, mother of demons…** Gazing into a mirror has catastrophic effects, by arousing the spirit *Asirta*, who in turn arouses a second spirit—who in turn arouses Lilith, associated with *Askara*, “Diphtheria.”

    On Lilith, see *Alfa Beita de-Ven Sira*, ed. Yassif, 231–34, 289–90; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-14b|1:14b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19b|19b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-34b|34b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-122a|122a]]–b, [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-131b|131b]], [[Zohar 11.09 - Sitrei Torah#^z1-148a|148a–b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-190b|190b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-204a|204a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-60b|2:60b–61a]], [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-96a|96a–b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-231b|231b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-242b|242b–244a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-19a|3:19a]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-69a|69a]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-76b|76b–77a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-224a|224a–b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231b|231b]], [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-266a|266a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-16c|16c]] (*MhN*); Trachtenberg, *Jewish Magic and Superstition*, 36–37; Margaliot, *Mal’akhei Elyon*, 235–41; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:464–65, 531; Patai, *The Hebrew Goddess*, 221–54; Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 356–61; Hutter, “Lilith”; Idel, *Kabbalah and Eros*, 120–22. On *Askara*, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-248b|2:248b]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 80 and n. 76).

    “Falling to the ground” may allude to epileptic seizures, which were thought to be brought on by certain phases of the moon. See Scholem. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-77a|3:77a]].

    On the general kabbalistic principle of “arousal below,” see note 411.

[^453]: ***I had to make sacrifices of well-being*…** The verse from Proverbs marks the beginning of the temptress’s speech of seduction to the lad devoid of sense. She informs him that, having just offered sacrifices (which have produced much meat for consumption), she has a rich banquet in store. See [[Torah 25 - Tzav#^7-16|Leviticus 7:16]].

    *Well-being* renders שלמים (*shelamim*), whose precise meaning is uncertain and which may imply “well-being, peace, greeting, repayment.” See Levine, *Leviticus*, 15; Milgrom, *Leviticus*, 1:204, 217–25.

    Here *shelamim* is connected with *shalom*, “peace,” and its unusual inflection in the plural (the suffix -*im*) is explained as pointing to its capacity to generate peace above and below, the left in harmony with the right. These sacrifices are thus “for me”—for the Accuser, to pacify her.

    On *shelamim* and *shalom*, see *Tosefta Zeyaḥim* 11:1; *Sifra, Nedavah* 16:1, 13a; *ḥovah* 4:2, 18c; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 9:9; *Tanḥuma, Toledot* 1, *Tsav* 4, 7; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Tsav* 10; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-11a|3:11a]], [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-12b|12b]].

[^454]: **As for me—since the day you were born…** The evil impulse appears from the moment of birth; the good impulse, from the age of thirteen (as signified by *on a long journey*).

    See [[Torah 02 - Noach#^8-21|Genesis 8:21]]; BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 91#91b|91b]]; *Berakhot* [[Berakhot 61#61a|61a]]; *Qiddushin* [[Kiddushin 30#30b|30b]]; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 16; B, 16, 30; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 4:13; *Midrash Tehillim* 9:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-78a|1:78a–79a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-110b|110b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-165b|165b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-179a|179a]]; [[Zohar 12.07 - Raza de-Razin#^z2-72b|2:72b]] (*RR*), [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-97b|97b–98a]], [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-113a|113a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-10c|10c]], [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-24b|24b]] (both *MhN*).

    On the temptress’s question “If now you do not delight yourself, then when?” cf. Hillel’s famous statement in M *Avot* 1:14: “If not now, when?”

[^455]: ***A bundle of desire he took with him*…** The phrase *tseror ha-kesef* means “bundle (or bag) of money,” though the *Zohar* is reading כסף (*kesef*), “money,” as “desire, longing”—another sense of the same root. Presumably, the subject “he” refers to the man cited in the previous verse, in which case the referent is the good impulse—which departs from a person while he delights in the world, returning on the day of judgment, Rosh Hashanah. Cf. *Matoq mi-Devash*, who understands the subject of the opening verse to be the evil impulse—which ascends empowered by a person’s desire, and leaves a person to their own evil devices.

    The full verse in Psalms reads: *Blast the ram’s horn on the new moon, ba-keseh, on the full moon, for our festival day*. The rare word *ba-keseh* (and *ha-kese*) apparently means *on the full moon*, but in rabbinic literature it is derived from the root כסה (*ksh*), “to cover,” and understood as referring to the new moon (specifically the new moon of Tishrei), when the moon’s face is almost totally concealed.

    On the word *ba-keseh*, see *Vayiqra Rabbah* 29:6; BT *Rosh ha-Shanah* [[Rosh Hashanah 8#8a|8a]]–b, 34a; *Beitsah* 16a; *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 11#11b|11b]], 96b; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 23:6; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 7; *Pesiqta Rabbati* 39, 166a; *Midrash Tehillim* 81:5; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-114b|1:114b]]; [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184a|2:184a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-98b|3:98b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-98b|98b–99a]] (*Piq*), [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-100b|100b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-231b|231b]], 275a.

[^456]: **Seventh hall…** The seventh hall on the side of holiness pertains to *Binah* (and *Malkhut*). Whereas *Binah* is often associated with the flow of wine, its counterpart on the side of impurity is the dregs of wine. Harsh forces of judgment manifest here—the squeezing of the grapes.

    On [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-21|Genesis 9:21]] describing Noah, see *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 23; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|140b]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-192a|192a]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-245a|2:245a]] (*Heikh*); *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.04 - Parashat Noah#^zh-22c|22c]] (*MhN*); Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 36–37 (43–44). On Eve’s squeezing grapes, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 19:5, in the name of Rabbi Aivu: “She squeezed grapes and offered them to him.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-36a|1:36a]], [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-192a|192a]]. According to *Bereshit Rabbah* 15:7 and BT *Sanhedrin* [[Sanhedrin 70#70a|70a]], Adam ate from the vine.

    The full verse from Psalms reads: *There is a cup in YHVH’s hand with foaming wine fully mixed; from this He pours; all the wicked of the earth drink, draining it to the very dregs*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-246b|2:246b]] (*Heikh*).

[^457]: **impure souls that descend…** Whereas holy souls and spirits originate from the seventh hall of holiness (see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-259a|2:259a]]; above, page 147), impure souls and spirits (of bastards) originate here.

    ממזר (*Mamzer*), “misbegotten,” technically, the offspring of an adulterous or incestuous union (not a child born out of wedlock). Here, the name *mamzer* is associated with *el zar*, “alien god,” signifying the demonic realm. As the father was immersed in the other side, so the child resulting from the forbidden union is contaminated by the other side. See JT *Qiddushin* 3:12, 64c; 4:14, 66b; *Kallah* 1:16; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^23-3|Deuteronomy 23:3]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118a|1:118a]] (*MhN*); cf. 2:113b.

[^458]: ***Tsofneyah tsafan ruaḥ, Restraining her is like restraining the wind*…** The verse from Proverbs refers to the contentious wife. The word רוח (*ruaḥ*) means both “spirit” and “wind.”

    In its simple sense, the clause from Joel means: *I will drive the northerner far from you*. According to BT *Sukkah* [[Sukkah 52#52a|52a]], *ha-tsefoni* is interpreted as *the hidden one*, alluding to the evil impulse.

    On the names of the halls and the names of the evil impulse, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-263a|2:263a]] (*Heikh*; above, p. 179 and n. 377).
